From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia


Organized collection of data in computing

This article is about the computing concept. For instances of the general concept,
see [Lists of databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").

[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
An [SQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") select statement and its result
In [computing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), a
**database** is an organized collection of [data](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or a type of [data store](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") based on the use of a
**database management system** (**DBMS**), the [software](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that interacts with [end users](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [applications](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and the database itself to capture and analyze the data. The DBMS additionally encompasses the core facilities provided to administer the database. The sum total of the database, the DBMS and the associated applications can be referred to as a
**database system**. Often the term "database" is also used loosely to refer to
any of the DBMS, the database system or an application associated with the database.

Small databases can be stored on a [file system](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), while large databases are hosted on [computer clusters](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or [cloud storage](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The [design of databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") spans formal techniques and practical considerations, including [data modeling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), efficient data representation and storage, [query languages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [privacy](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") of sensitive data, and [distributed computing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") issues, including supporting [concurrent](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") access and [fault tolerance](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").

[Computer scientists](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") may classify database management systems according to the [database models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that they support. [Relational databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") became dominant in the 1980s. These model data as [rows](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [columns](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") in a series of [tables](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and the vast majority use [SQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") for writing and querying data. In the 2000s, non-relational databases became popular, collectively referred to as [NoSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), because they use different [query languages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").


## Terminology

Formally, a **database** refers to a set of related data accessed through the use
of a **database management system** (DBMS), which is an integrated set of [computer software](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that allows [users](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to interact with one or more databases and provides access to all of the data contained in the database (although restrictions may exist that limit access to particular data). The DBMS provides various functions that allow entry, storage and retrieval of large quantities of information and provides ways to manage how that information is organized.

Because of the close relationship between them, the term "database" is often used
casually to refer to both a database and the DBMS used to manipulate it.

Outside the world of professional [information technology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the term
**database** is often used to refer to any collection of related data (such as a
[spreadsheet](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or a card index) as size and usage requirements typically necessitate use of a database management
system.[[1]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Existing DBMSs provide various functions that allow management of a database and
its data. *Data definition* entails creation, modification and removal of the structures
in the database which specify the data's organization.[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]
*Updates* include insertion, modification, and deletion of the data itself.[[2]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
*Retrieval* is the selection of data according to specified criteria.[[3]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Database *Administration* involves registering users, enforcing data security, monitoring
performance, maintaining data integrity, dealing with concurrency control, and recovering
corrupted information.[[4]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Both a database and its DBMS conform to the principles of a particular [database model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").[[5]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
**Database system** refers collectively to the database model, database management
system, and database.[[6]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Physically, database [servers](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") are dedicated computers that hold the actual databases and run only the DBMS and related
software.[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]

Databases and DBMSs can be categorized according to the database model(s) that they
support (such as [relational](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or [XML](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), the type(s) of computer they run on (from a [server cluster](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to a [mobile phone](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), the [query language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")(s) used to access the database (such as SQL or [XQuery](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), and their internal engineering, which affects performance, [scalability](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), resilience, and
security.[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]


## History

The sizes, capabilities, and performance of databases and their respective DBMSs
have grown in orders of magnitude. These performance increases were enabled by the
technology progress in the areas of [processors](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [computer memory](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [computer storage](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and [computer networks](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The concept of a database was made possible by the emergence of direct access [storage media](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") such as [magnetic disks](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard"), which became widely available in the mid-1960s; earlier systems relied on sequential storage of data on [magnetic tape](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard"). The subsequent development of database technology can be divided into three eras based on data model or structure: [navigational](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
"ext.globalCssJs.user.styles"),[[7]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")
SQL/[relational](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles"), and post-relational.

The two main early navigational [data models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") were the [hierarchical model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and the [CODASYL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") model ([network model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")). These were characterized by the use of [pointers](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (often physical disk addresses) to follow relationships from one record to another.

The [relational model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), first proposed in 1970 by [Edgar F. Codd](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), departed from this tradition by insisting that [applications](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") should search for data by content, rather than by following links. The relational model employs sets of ledger-style tables, each used for a different type of [entity](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Only in the mid-1980s did computing hardware become powerful enough to allow the wide deployment of relational systems (DBMSs plus applications). By the early 1990s, however, relational systems dominated in all large-scale [data processing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") applications, and
as of 2018[[update]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[IBM Db2](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [Oracle](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [MySQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and [Microsoft SQL Server](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") are the most searched [DBMS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").[[8]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The dominant database language, standardized SQL for the relational model, has influenced
database languages for other data models.[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]

[Object databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") were developed in the 1980s to overcome the inconvenience of [object–relational impedance mismatch](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), which led to the coining of the term "post-relational" and also the development of hybrid [object–relational databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").

The next generation of post-relational databases in the late 2000s became known as
[NoSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") databases, introducing fast [key–value stores](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [document-oriented databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). A competing "next generation" known as [NewSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") databases attempted new implementations that retained the relational/SQL model while aiming to match the high performance of NoSQL compared to commercially available relational DBMSs.


### 1960s, navigational DBMS

Further information: [Navigational database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Basic structure of navigational [CODASYL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") database model
The introduction of the term *database* coincided with the availability of direct-access
storage (disks and drums) from the mid-1960s onwards. The term represented a contrast
with the tape-based systems of the past, allowing shared interactive use rather than
daily [batch processing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The [Oxford English Dictionary](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") cites a 1962 report by the [System Development Corporation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") of California as the first to use the term "data-base" in a specific technical
sense.[[9]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

As computers grew in speed and capability, a number of general-purpose database systems
emerged; by the mid-1960s a number of such systems had come into commercial use.
Interest in a standard began to grow, and [Charles Bachman](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), author of one such product, the [Integrated Data Store](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (IDS), founded the [Database Task Group](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") within [CODASYL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the group responsible for the creation and standardization of [COBOL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). In 1971, the Database Task Group delivered their standard, which generally became known as the
*CODASYL approach*, and soon a number of commercial products based on this approach
entered the market.

The CODASYL approach offered applications the ability to navigate around a linked
data set which was formed into a large network. Applications could find records by
one of three methods:

1. Use of a primary key (known as a CALC key, typically implemented by [hashing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("))
2. Navigating relationships (called *sets*) from one record to another
3. Scanning all the records in a sequential order


Later systems added [B-trees](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to provide alternate access paths. Many CODASYL databases also added a declarative query language for end users (as distinct from the navigational [API](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")). However, CODASYL databases were complex and required significant training and effort to produce useful applications.

[IBM](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") also had its own DBMS in 1966, known as [Information Management System](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (IMS). IMS was a development of software written for the [Apollo program](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") on the [System/360](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). IMS was generally similar in concept to CODASYL, but used a strict hierarchy for its model of data navigation instead of CODASYL's network model. Both concepts later became known as navigational databases due to the way data was accessed: the term was popularized by Bachman's 1973 [Turing Award](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") presentation
*The Programmer as Navigator*. IMS is classified by IBM as a [hierarchical database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). IDMS and [Cincom Systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")' [TOTAL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") databases are classified as network databases. IMS remains in use
as of 2014[[update]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[10]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")


### 1970s, relational DBMS

[Edgar F. Codd](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") worked at IBM in [San Jose, California](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), in an office primarily involved in the development of [hard disk](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
systems.[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
He was unhappy with the navigational model of the CODASYL approach, notably the lack
of a "search" facility. In 1970, he wrote a number of papers that outlined a new
approach to database construction that eventually culminated in the groundbreaking
*A Relational Model of Data for Large Shared Data Banks*.[[12]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

The paper described a new system for storing and working with large databases. Instead
of records being stored in some sort of [linked list](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") of free-form records as in CODASYL, Codd's idea was to organize the data as a number of "[tables](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")", each table being used for a different type of entity. Each table would contain a fixed number of columns containing the attributes of the entity. One or more columns of each table were designated as a  [primary key](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") by which the rows of the table could be uniquely identified; cross-references between tables always used these primary keys, rather than disk addresses, and queries would join tables based on these key relationships, using a set of operations based on the mathematical system of [relational calculus](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (from which the model takes its name). Splitting the data into a set of normalized tables (or
*relations*) aimed to ensure that each "fact" was only stored once, thus simplifying
update operations. Virtual tables called *views* could present the data in different
ways for different users, but views could not be directly updated.

Codd used mathematical terms to define the model: relations, tuples, and domains
rather than tables, rows, and columns. The terminology that is now familiar came
from early implementations. Codd would later criticize the tendency for practical
implementations to depart from the mathematical foundations on which the model was
based.
[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
In the [relational model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), records are "linked" using virtual keys not stored in the database but defined as needed between the data contained in the records.

The use of primary keys (user-oriented identifiers) to represent cross-table relationships,
rather than disk addresses, had two primary motivations. From an engineering perspective,
it enabled tables to be relocated and resized without expensive database reorganization.
But Codd was more interested in the difference in semantics: the use of explicit
identifiers made it easier to define update operations with clean mathematical definitions,
and it also enabled query operations to be defined in terms of the established discipline
of [first-order predicate calculus](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("); because these operations have clean mathematical properties, it becomes possible to rewrite queries in provably correct ways, which is the basis of query optimization. There is no loss of expressiveness compared with the hierarchic or network models, though the connections between tables are no longer so explicit.

In the hierarchic and network models, records were allowed to have a complex internal
structure. For example, the salary history of an employee might be represented as
a "repeating group" within the employee record. In the relational model, the process
of normalization led to such internal structures being replaced by data held in multiple
tables, connected only by logical keys.

For instance, a common use of a database system is to track information about users,
their name, login information, various addresses and phone numbers. In the navigational
approach, all of this data would be placed in a single variable-length record. In
the relational approach, the data would be *normalized* into a user table, an address
table and a phone number table (for instance). Records would be created in these
optional tables only if the address or phone numbers were actually provided.

As well as identifying rows/records using logical identifiers rather than disk addresses,
Codd changed the way in which applications assembled data from multiple records.
Rather than requiring applications to gather data one record at a time by navigating
the links, they would use a declarative query language that expressed what data was
required, rather than the access path by which it should be found. Finding an efficient
access path to the data became the responsibility of the database management system,
rather than the application programmer. This process, called query optimization,
depended on the fact that queries were expressed in terms of mathematical logic.

Codd's paper inspired teams at various universities to research the subject, including
one at [University of California, Berkeley](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
led by [Eugene Wong](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [Michael Stonebraker](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), who started [INGRES](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") using funding that had already been allocated for a geographical database project and student programmers to produce code. Beginning in 1973, INGRES delivered its first test products which were generally ready for widespread use in 1979. INGRES was similar to [System R](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") in a number of ways, including the use of a "language" for [data access](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), known as [QUEL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Over time, INGRES moved to the emerging SQL standard.

IBM itself did one test implementation of the relational model, [PRTV](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and a production one, [Business System 12](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), both now discontinued. [Honeywell](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") wrote [MRDS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") for [Multics](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and now there are two new implementations: [Alphora Dataphor](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and Rel. Most other DBMS implementations usually called
*relational* are actually SQL DBMSs.

In 1970, the University of Michigan began development of the [MICRO Information Management System](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[13]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
based on [D.L. Childs](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")' Set-Theoretic Data
model.[[14]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[15]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[16]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The university in 1974 hosted a debate between Codd and Bachman which Bruce Lindsay
of IBM later described as "throwing lightning bolts at each other!".[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
MICRO was used to manage very large data sets by the [US Department of Labor](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the [U.S. Environmental Protection Agency](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and researchers from the [University of Alberta](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the [University of Michigan](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and [Wayne State University](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). It ran on IBM mainframe computers using the [Michigan Terminal System](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").[[17]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The system remained in production until 1998.


### Integrated approach

Main article: [Database machine](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
In the 1970s and 1980s, attempts were made to build database systems with integrated
hardware and software. The underlying philosophy was that such integration would
provide higher performance at a lower cost. Examples were [IBM System/38](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the early offering of [Teradata](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and the [Britton Lee, Inc.](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") database machine.

Another approach to hardware support for database management was [ICL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")'s [CAFS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") accelerator, a hardware disk controller with programmable search capabilities. In the long term, these efforts were generally unsuccessful because specialized database machines could not keep pace with the rapid development and progress of general-purpose computers. Thus most database systems nowadays are software systems running on general-purpose hardware, using general-purpose computer data storage. However, this idea is still pursued in certain applications by some companies like [Netezza](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and Oracle ([Exadata](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")).


### Late 1970s, SQL DBMS

IBM formed a team led by Codd that started working on a prototype system, *[System R](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*
despite opposition from others at the company.[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The first version was ready in 1974/5, and work then started on multi-table systems
in which the data could be split so that all of the data for a record (some of which
is optional) did not have to be stored in a single large "chunk". Subsequent multi-user
versions were tested by customers in 1978 and 1979, by which time a standardized
[query language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") –
SQL[[18]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[*[citation
needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]
– had been added. Codd's ideas were establishing themselves as both workable and
superior to CODASYL, pushing IBM to develop a true production version of System R,
known as *SQL/DS*, and, later, *Database 2* ([IBM Db2](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")).

[Larry Ellison](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")'s Oracle Database (or more simply, [Oracle](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")) started from a different chain, based on IBM's papers on System R. Though Oracle V1 implementations were completed in 1978, it was not until Oracle Version 2 when Ellison beat IBM to market in
1979.[[19]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Stonebraker went on to apply the lessons from INGRES to develop a new database, Postgres,
which is now known as [PostgreSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). PostgreSQL is often used for global mission-critical applications (the .org and .info domain name registries use it as their primary [data store](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), as do many large companies and financial institutions).

In Sweden, Codd's paper was also read and [Mimer SQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") was developed in the mid-1970s at [Uppsala University](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). In 1984, this project was consolidated into an independent enterprise.

Another data model, the [entity–relationship model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), emerged in 1976 and gained popularity for [database design](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") as it emphasized a more familiar description than the earlier relational model. Later on, entity–relationship constructs were retrofitted as a [data modeling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") construct for the relational model, and the difference between the two has become
irrelevant.[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*]


### 1980s, on the desktop

Besides IBM and various software companies such as [Sybase](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [Informix Corporation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), most large computer hardware vendors by the 1980s had their own database systems such as [DEC](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")'s [VAX Rdb/VMS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").[[20]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The decade ushered in the age of [desktop computing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The new computers empowered their users with spreadsheets like [Lotus 1-2-3](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and database software like [dBASE](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The dBASE product was lightweight and easy for any computer user to understand out of the box. [C. Wayne Ratliff](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the creator of dBASE, stated: "dBASE was different from programs like BASIC, C, FORTRAN, and COBOL in that a lot of the dirty work had already been done. The data manipulation is done by dBASE instead of by the user, so the user can concentrate on what he is doing, rather than having to mess with the dirty details of opening, reading, and closing files, and managing space
allocation."[[21]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
dBASE was one of the top selling software titles in the 1980s and early 1990s.


### 1990s, object-oriented

By the start of the decade databases had become a billion-dollar industry in about
ten years.[[20]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The 1990s, along with a rise in [object-oriented programming](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), saw a growth in how data in various databases were handled. Programmers and designers began to treat the data in their databases as [objects](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). That is to say that if a person's data were in a database, that person's attributes, such as their address, phone number, and age, were now considered to belong to that person instead of being extraneous data. This allows for relations between data to be related to objects and their [attributes](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and not to individual
fields.[[22]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The term "[object–relational impedance mismatch](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")" described the inconvenience of translating between programmed objects and database tables. [Object databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [object–relational databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") attempt to solve this problem by providing an object-oriented language (sometimes as extensions to SQL) that programmers can use as alternative to purely relational SQL. On the programming side, libraries known as [object–relational mappings](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (ORMs) attempt to solve the same problem.


### 2000s, NoSQL and NewSQL

Main articles: [NoSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [NewSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Database sales grew rapidly during the [dotcom bubble](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and, after its end, the rise of [ecommerce](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The popularity of [open source](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") databases such as [MySQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") has grown since 2000, to the extent that Ken Jacobs of Oracle said in 2005 that perhaps "these guys are doing to us what we did to
IBM".[[20]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

[XML databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") are a type of structured document-oriented database that allows querying based on [XML](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") document attributes. XML databases are mostly used in applications where the data is conveniently viewed as a collection of documents, with a structure that can vary from the very flexible to the highly rigid: examples include scientific articles, patents, tax filings, and personnel records.

[NoSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") databases are often very
fast,[[23]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[24]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
do not require fixed table schemas, avoid join operations by storing [denormalized](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") data, and are designed to [scale horizontally](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Some NoSQL databases, including graph databases such as [Neo4j](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), represent data as nodes and edges to model relationships between
entities.[[25]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

In recent years, there has been a strong demand for massively distributed databases
with high partition tolerance, but according to the [CAP theorem](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), it is impossible for a [distributed system](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to simultaneously provide [consistency](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), availability, and partition tolerance guarantees. A distributed system can satisfy any two of these guarantees at the same time, but not all three. For that reason, many NoSQL databases are using what is called [eventual consistency](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to provide both availability and partition tolerance guarantees with a reduced level of data consistency.

[NewSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is a class of modern relational databases that aims to provide the same scalable performance of NoSQL systems for online transaction processing (read-write) workloads while still using SQL and maintaining the [ACID](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") guarantees of a traditional database system.


## Use cases

|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| This section **does not [cite](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") any [sources](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")**. Please help [improve this section](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") by [adding citations to reliable sources](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").  *(March 2013)** ([Learn how and when to remove this message](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("))* |

Databases are used to support internal operations of organizations and to underpin
online interactions with customers and suppliers (see [Enterprise software](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")).

Databases are used to hold administrative information and more specialized data,
such as engineering data or economic models. Examples include computerized [library](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") systems, [flight reservation systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), computerized [parts inventory systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and many [content management systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that store [websites](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") as collections of webpages in a database.


## Classification terms

One way to classify databases involves the type of their contents, for example: [bibliographic](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard"), document-text, statistical, or multimedia objects. Another way is by their application area, for example: accounting, music compositions, movies, banking, manufacturing, or insurance. A third way is by some technical aspect, such as the database structure or interface type. This section lists a few of the adjectives used to characterize different kinds of databases.

- An [in-memory database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") is a database that primarily resides in [main memory](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles"), but is typically backed-up by non-volatile computer data storage. Main memory databases are faster than disk databases, and so are often used where response time is critical, such as in telecommunications network equipment.
- An [active database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") includes an event-driven architecture which can respond to conditions both inside and outside the database. Possible uses include security monitoring, alerting, statistics gathering and authorization. Many databases provide active database features in the form of [database triggers](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles").
- A [cloud database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") relies on [cloud technology](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Both the database and most of its DBMS reside remotely, "in the cloud", while its applications are both developed by programmers and later maintained and used by end-users through a [web browser](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [Open APIs](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").
- [Data warehouses](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[*[citation needed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*] archive data from operational databases and often from external sources such as market research firms. The warehouse becomes the central source of data for use by managers and other end-users who may not have access to operational data.
