For example, sales data might be aggregated to weekly totals and converted from internal product codes to use [UPCs](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") so that they can be compared with [ACNielsen](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") data. Some basic and essential components of data warehousing include extracting, analyzing, and [mining](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") data, transforming, loading, and managing data so as to make them available for further use.
- A [deductive database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") combines [logic programming](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") with a relational database.
- A [distributed database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is one in which both the data and the DBMS span multiple computers.
- A [document-oriented database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is designed for storing, retrieving, and managing document-oriented, or semi structured, information. Document-oriented databases are one of the main categories of NoSQL databases.
- An [embedded database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") system is a DBMS which is tightly integrated with an application software that requires access to stored data in such a way that the DBMS is hidden from the application's end-users and requires little or no ongoing maintenance.[[26]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- End-user databases consist of data developed by individual end-users. Examples of these are collections of documents, spreadsheets, presentations, multimedia, and other files. Several products[*[which?](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*] exist to support such databases.
- A [federated database system](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") comprises several distinct databases, each with its own DBMS. It is handled as a single database by a federated database management system (FDBMS), which transparently integrates multiple autonomous DBMSs, possibly of different types (in which case it would also be a [heterogeneous database system](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), and provides them with an integrated conceptual view.
- Sometimes the term *multi-database* is used as a synonym for federated database, though it may refer to a less integrated (e.g., without an FDBMS and a managed integrated schema) group of databases that cooperate in a single application. In this case, typically [middleware](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is used for distribution, which typically includes an atomic commit protocol (ACP), e.g., the [two-phase commit protocol](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), to allow [distributed (global) transactions](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") across the participating databases.
- A [graph database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is a kind of NoSQL database that uses [graph structures](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") with nodes, edges, and properties to represent and store information. General graph databases that can store any graph are distinct from specialized graph databases such as [triplestores](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [network databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").
- An [array DBMS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is a kind of NoSQL DBMS that allows modeling, storage, and retrieval of (usually large) multi-dimensional [arrays](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") such as satellite images and climate simulation output.
- In a [hypertext](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or [hypermedia](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") database, any word or a piece of text representing an object, e.g., another piece of text, an article, a picture, or a film, can be [hyperlinked](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to that object. Hypertext databases are particularly useful for organizing large amounts of disparate information. For example, they are useful for organizing [online encyclopedias](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), where users can conveniently jump around the text. The [World Wide Web](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is thus a large distributed hypertext database.
- A [knowledge base](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (abbreviated **KB**, **kb** or Δ[[27]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[28]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")) is a special kind of database for [knowledge management](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), providing the means for the computerized collection, organization, and [retrieval](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") of [knowledge](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Also a collection of data representing problems with their solutions and related experiences.

- A [mobile database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") can be carried on or synchronized from a mobile computing device.
- [Operational databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") store detailed data about the operations of an organization. They typically process relatively high volumes of updates using [transactions](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Examples include [customer databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that record contact, credit, and demographic information about a business's customers, personnel databases that hold information such as salary, benefits, skills data about employees, [enterprise resource planning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") systems that record details about product components, parts inventory, and financial databases that keep track of the organization's money, accounting and financial dealings.
- A [parallel database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") seeks to improve performance through [parallelization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") for tasks such as loading data, building indexes and evaluating queries.
The major parallel DBMS architectures which are induced by the underlying [hardware](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") architecture are:
- **[Shared memory architecture](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")**, where multiple processors share the main memory space, as well as other data storage.
- **Shared disk architecture**, where each processing unit (typically consisting of multiple processors) has its own main memory, but all units share the other storage.
- **[Shared-nothing architecture](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")**, where each processing unit has its own main memory and other storage.

- [Probabilistic databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") employ [fuzzy logic](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to draw inferences from imprecise data.
- [Real-time databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") process transactions fast enough for the result to come back and be acted on right away.
- A [spatial database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") can store the data with multidimensional features. The queries on such data include location-based queries, like "Where is the closest hotel in my area?".
- A [temporal database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") has built-in time aspects, for example a temporal data model and a temporal version of [SQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). More specifically the temporal aspects usually include valid-time and transaction-time.
- A [terminology-oriented database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") builds upon an [object-oriented database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), often customized for a specific field.
- An [unstructured data](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") database is intended to store in a manageable and protected way diverse objects that do not fit naturally and conveniently in common databases. It may include email messages, documents, journals, multimedia objects, etc. The name may be misleading since some objects can be highly structured. However, the entire possible object collection does not fit into a predefined structured framework. Most established DBMSs now support unstructured data in various ways, and new dedicated DBMSs are emerging.


## Database management system

Connolly and Begg define database management system (DBMS) as a "software system
that enables users to define, create, maintain and control access to the database."[[29]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Examples of DBMS include [MySQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [MariaDB](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [PostgreSQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [Microsoft SQL Server](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [Oracle Database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and [Microsoft Access](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").

The DBMS acronym is sometimes extended to indicate the underlying [database model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), with RDBMS for the [relational](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), OODBMS for the [object (oriented)](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and ORDBMS for the [object–relational model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Other extensions can indicate some other characteristics, such as DDBMS for a distributed database management systems.

The functionality provided by a DBMS can vary enormously. The core functionality
is the storage, retrieval and update of data. [Codd](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") proposed the following functions and services a fully-fledged general purpose DBMS should
provide:[[30]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

- Data storage, retrieval and update
- User accessible catalog or [data dictionary](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") describing the metadata
- Support for transactions and concurrency
- Facilities for recovering the database should it become damaged
- Support for authorization of access and update of data
- Access support from remote locations
- Enforcing constraints to ensure data in the database abides by certain rules

It is also generally to be expected the DBMS will provide a set of utilities for
such purposes as may be necessary to administer the database effectively, including
import, export, monitoring, defragmentation and analysis utilities.[[31]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The core part of the DBMS interacting between the database and the application interface
sometimes referred to as the [database engine](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").

Often DBMSs will have configuration parameters that can be statically and dynamically
tuned, for example the maximum amount of main memory on a server the database can
use. The trend is to minimize the amount of manual configuration, and for cases such
as [embedded databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") the need to target zero-administration is paramount.

The large major enterprise DBMSs have tended to increase in size and functionality
and have involved up to thousands of human and years of development effort throughout
their lifetime.[[a]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Early multi-user DBMS typically only allowed for the application to reside on the
same computer with access via [terminals](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or terminal emulation software. The [client–server architecture](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") was a development where the application resided on a client desktop and the database on a server allowing the processing to be distributed. This evolved into a [multitier architecture](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") incorporating [application servers](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [web servers](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") with the end user interface via a [web browser](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") with the database only directly connected to the adjacent
tier.[[33]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

A general-purpose DBMS will provide public [application programming interfaces](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (API) and optionally a processor for [database languages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") such as [SQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to allow applications to be written to interact with and manipulate the database. A special purpose DBMS may use a private API and be specifically customized and linked to a single application. For example, an [email](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") system performs many of the functions of a general-purpose DBMS such as message insertion, message deletion, attachment handling, blocklist lookup, associating messages an email address and so forth however these functions are limited to what is required to handle email.


## Application

Main article: [Database application](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
External interaction with the database will be via an application program that interfaces
with the DBMS.[[34]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
This can range from a [database tool](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that allows users to execute SQL queries textually or graphically, to a website that happens to use a database to store and search information.


### Application program interface

A [programmer](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") will [code](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") interactions to the database (sometimes referred to as a [datasource](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")) via an [application program interface](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (API) or via a [database language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The particular API or language chosen will need to be supported by DBMS, possibly indirectly via a [preprocessor](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or a bridging API. Some API's aim to be database independent, [ODBC](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") being a commonly known example. Other common API's include [JDBC](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [ADO.NET](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").


## Database languages

Database languages are special-purpose languages, which allow one or more of the
following tasks, sometimes distinguished as [sublanguages](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("):

- [Data control language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (DCL) – controls access to data;
- [Data definition language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (DDL) – defines data types such as creating, altering, or dropping tables and the relationships among them;
- [Data manipulation language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (DML) – performs tasks such as inserting, updating, or deleting data occurrences;
- [Data query language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (DQL) – allows searching for information and computing derived information.

Database languages are specific to a particular data model. Notable examples include:

- SQL combines the roles of data definition, data manipulation, and query in a single language. It was one of the first commercial languages for the relational model, although it departs in some respects from [the relational model as described by Codd](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (for example, the rows and columns of a table can be ordered). SQL became a standard of the [American National Standards Institute](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (ANSI) in 1986, and of the [International Organization for Standardization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (ISO) in 1987. The standards have been regularly enhanced since and are supported (with varying degrees of conformance) by all mainstream commercial relational DBMSs.[[35]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[[36]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [OQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is an object model language standard (from the [Object Data Management Group](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")). It has influenced the design of some of the newer query languages like [JDOQL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [EJB QL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").
- [XQuery](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") is a standard XML query language implemented by XML database systems such as [MarkLogic](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [eXist](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), by relational databases with XML capability such as Oracle and Db2, and also by in-memory XML processors such as [Saxon](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").
- [SQL/XML](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") combines [XQuery](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") with SQL.[[37]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

A database language may also incorporate features like:

- DBMS-specific configuration and storage engine management
- Computations to modify query results, like counting, summing, averaging, sorting, grouping, and cross-referencing
- Constraint enforcement (e.g. in an automotive database, only allowing one engine type per car)
- Application programming interface version of the query language, for programmer convenience


## Storage

Main articles: [Computer data storage](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [Database engine](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Database storage is the container of the physical materialization of a database.
It comprises the *internal* (physical) *level* in the database architecture. It also
contains all the information needed (e.g., [metadata](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), "data about the data", and internal [data structures](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")) to reconstruct the
*conceptual level* and *external level* from the internal level when needed. Databases
as digital objects contain three layers of information which must be stored: the
data, the structure, and the semantics. Proper storage of all three layers is needed
for future [preservation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and longevity of the
database.[[38]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Putting data into permanent storage is generally the responsibility of the [database engine](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") a.k.a. "storage engine". Though typically accessed by a DBMS through the underlying operating system (and often using the operating systems' [file systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") as intermediates for storage layout), storage properties and configuration settings are extremely important for the efficient operation of the DBMS, and thus are closely maintained by database administrators. A DBMS, while in operation, always has its database residing in several types of storage (e.g., memory and external storage). The database data and the additional needed information, possibly in very large amounts, are coded into bits. Data typically reside in the storage in structures that look completely different from the way the data look at the conceptual and external levels, but in ways that attempt to optimize (the best possible) these levels' reconstruction when needed by users and programs, as well as for computing additional types of needed information from the data (e.g., when querying the database).

Some DBMSs support specifying which [character encoding](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") was used to store data, so multiple encodings can be used in the same database.

Various low-level database storage structures are used by the storage engine to serialize
the data model so it can be written to the medium of choice. Techniques such as indexing
may be used to improve performance. Conventional storage is row-oriented, but there
are also [column-oriented](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [correlation databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").


### Materialized views

Main article: [Materialized view](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Often storage redundancy is employed to increase performance. A common example is
storing *materialized views*, which consist of frequently needed *external views*
or query results. Storing such views saves the expensive computing them each time
they are needed. The downsides of materialized views are the overhead incurred when
updating them to keep them synchronized with their original updated database data,
and the cost of storage redundancy.


### Replication

See also: [Replication (computing) § Database replication](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Occasionally a database employs storage redundancy by [database objects](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") replication (with one or more copies) to increase data availability (both to improve performance of simultaneous multiple end-user accesses to the same database object, and to provide resiliency in a case of partial failure of a distributed database). Updates of a replicated object need to be synchronized across the object copies. In many cases, the entire database is replicated.


### Virtualization

With [data virtualization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the data used remains in its original locations and real-time access is established to allow analytics across multiple sources. This can aid in resolving some technical difficulties such as compatibility problems when combining data from various platforms, lowering the risk of error caused by faulty data, and guaranteeing that the newest data is used. Furthermore, avoiding the creation of a new database containing personal information can make it easier to comply with privacy regulations. However, with data virtualization, the connection to all necessary data sources must be operational as there is no local copy of the data, which is one of the main drawbacks of the
approach.[[39]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")


## Security

Main article: [Database security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------||
| This article **appears to contradict the article *[Database security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")***. Please discuss at the [talk page](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and do not remove this message until the contradictions are resolved.  *(March 2013)* |

[Database security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") deals with all various aspects of protecting the database content, its owners, and its users. It ranges from protection from intentional unauthorized database uses to unintentional database accesses by unauthorized entities (e.g., a person or a computer program).

Database access control deals with controlling who (a person or a certain computer
program) are allowed to access what information in the database. The information
may comprise specific database objects (e.g., record types, specific records, data
structures), certain computations over certain objects (e.g., query types, or specific
queries), or using specific access paths to the former (e.g., using specific indexes
or other data structures to access information). Database access controls are set
by special authorized (by the database owner) personnel that uses dedicated protected
security DBMS interfaces.

This may be managed directly on an individual basis, or by the assignment of individuals
and [privileges](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to groups, or (in the most elaborate models) through the assignment of individuals and groups to roles which are then granted entitlements. Data security prevents unauthorized users from viewing or updating the database. Using passwords, users are allowed access to the entire database or subsets of it called "subschemas". For example, an employee database can contain all the data about an individual employee, but one group of users may be authorized to view only payroll data, while others are allowed access to only work history and medical data. If the DBMS provides a way to interactively enter and update the database, as well as interrogate it, this capability allows for managing personal databases.

[Data security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") in general deals with protecting specific chunks of data, both physically (i.e., from corruption, or destruction, or removal; e.g., see [physical security](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), or the interpretation of them, or parts of them to meaningful information (e.g., by looking at the strings of bits that they comprise, concluding specific valid credit-card numbers; e.g., see [data encryption](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")).

Change and access logging records who accessed which attributes, what was changed,
and when it was changed. Logging services allow for a forensic [database audit](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") later by keeping a record of access occurrences and changes. Sometimes application-level code is used to record changes rather than leaving this in the database. Monitoring can be set up to attempt to detect security breaches. Therefore, organizations must take database security seriously because of the many benefits it provides. Organizations will be safeguarded from security breaches and hacking activities like firewall intrusion, virus spread, and ransom ware. This helps in protecting the company's essential information, which cannot be shared with outsiders at any
cause.[[40]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")


## Transactions and concurrency

Further information: [Concurrency control](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
[Database transactions](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") can be used to introduce some level of [fault tolerance](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and [data integrity](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") after recovery from a [crash](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). A database transaction is a unit of work, typically encapsulating a number of operations over a database (e.g., reading a database object, writing, acquiring or releasing a [lock](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), etc.), an abstraction supported in database and also other systems. Each transaction has well defined boundaries in terms of which program/code executions are included in that transaction (determined by the transaction's programmer via special transaction commands).

The acronym [ACID](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") describes some ideal properties of a database transaction: [atomicity](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [consistency](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), [isolation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and [durability](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(").


## Migration

See also: [Data migration § Database migration](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
A database built with one DBMS is not portable to another DBMS (i.e., the other DBMS
cannot run it). However, in some situations, it is desirable to migrate a database
from one DBMS to another. The reasons are primarily economical (different DBMSs may
have different [total costs of ownership](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") or TCOs), functional, and operational (different DBMSs may have different capabilities). The migration involves the database's transformation from one DBMS type to another. The transformation should maintain (if possible) the database related application (i.e., all related application programs) intact. Thus, the database's conceptual and external architectural levels should be maintained in the transformation. It may be desired that also some aspects of the architecture internal level are maintained. A complex or large database migration may be a complicated and costly (one-time) project by itself, which should be factored into the decision to migrate. This is in spite of the fact that tools may exist to help migration between specific DBMSs. Typically, a DBMS vendor provides tools to help import databases from other popular DBMSs.


## Building, maintaining, and tuning

Main article: [Database tuning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard")
After designing a database for an application, the next stage is building the database.
Typically, an appropriate general-purpose DBMS can be selected to be used for this
purpose. A DBMS provides the needed [user interfaces](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") to be used by database administrators to define the needed application's data structures within the DBMS's respective data model. Other user interfaces are used to select needed DBMS parameters (like security related, storage allocation parameters, etc.).

When the database is ready (all its data structures and other needed components are
defined), it is typically populated with initial application's data (database initialization,
which is typically a distinct project; in many cases using specialized DBMS interfaces
that support bulk insertion) before making it operational. In some cases, the database
becomes operational while empty of application data, and data are accumulated during
its operation.

After the database is created, initialized and populated it needs to be maintained.
Various database parameters may need changing and the database may need to be tuned
([tuning](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")) for better performance; application's data structures may be changed or added, new related application programs may be written to add to the application's functionality, etc.


## Backup and restore

Main article: [Backup](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Sometimes it is desired to bring a database back to a previous state (for many reasons,
e.g., cases when the database is found corrupted due to a software error, or if it
has been updated with erroneous data). To achieve this, a backup operation is done
occasionally or continuously, where each desired database state (i.e., the values
of its data and their embedding in database's data structures) is kept within dedicated
backup files (many techniques exist to do this effectively). When it is decided by
a database administrator to bring the database back to this state (e.g., by specifying
this state by a desired point in time when the database was in this state), these
files are used to restore that state.


## Static analysis

Static analysis techniques for software verification can be applied also in the scenario
of query languages. In particular, the \*[Abstract interpretation](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") framework has been extended to the field of query languages for relational databases as a way to support sound approximation
techniques.[[41]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The semantics of query languages can be tuned according to suitable abstractions
of the concrete domain of data. The abstraction of relational database systems has
many interesting applications, in particular, for security purposes, such as fine-grained
access control, watermarking, etc.


## Miscellaneous features

Other DBMS features might include:

- [Database logs](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") – This helps in keeping a history of the executed functions.
- Graphics component for producing graphs and charts, especially in a data warehouse system.
- [Query optimizer](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") – Performs query optimization on every query to choose an efficient *[query plan](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")* (a partial order (tree) of operations) to be executed to compute the query result. May be specific to a particular storage engine.
- Tools or hooks for database design, application programming, application program maintenance, database performance analysis and monitoring, database configuration monitoring, DBMS hardware configuration (a DBMS and related database may span computers, networks, and storage units) and related database mapping (especially for a distributed DBMS), storage allocation and database layout monitoring, storage migration, etc.

Increasingly, there are calls for a single system that incorporates all of these
core functionalities into the same build, test, and deployment framework for database
management and source control. Borrowing from other developments in the software
industry, some market such offerings as "[DevOps](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") for
database".[[42]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")


## Design and modeling

Main article: [Database design](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

The first task of a database designer is to produce a [conceptual data model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that reflects the structure of the information to be held in the database. A common approach to this is to develop an [entity–relationship model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), often with the aid of drawing tools. Another popular approach is the [Unified Modeling Language](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). A successful data model will accurately reflect the possible state of the external world being modeled: for example, if people can have more than one phone number, it will allow this information to be captured. Designing a good conceptual data model requires a good understanding of the application domain; it typically involves asking deep questions about the things of interest to an organization, like "can a customer also be a supplier?", or "if a product is sold with two different forms of packaging, are those the same product or different products?", or "if a plane flies from New York to Dubai via Frankfurt, is that one flight or two (or maybe even three)?". The answers to these questions establish definitions of the terminology used for entities (customers, products, flights, flight segments) and their relationships and attributes.

Producing the conceptual data model sometimes involves input from [business processes](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), or the analysis of [workflow](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") in the organization. This can help to establish what information is needed in the database, and what can be left out. For example, it can help when deciding whether the database needs to hold historic data as well as current data.

Having produced a conceptual data model that users are happy with, the next stage
is to translate this into a [schema](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") that implements the relevant data structures within the database. This process is often called logical database design, and the output is a [logical data model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") expressed in the form of a schema. Whereas the conceptual data model is (in theory at least) independent of the choice of database technology, the logical data model will be expressed in terms of a particular database model supported by the chosen DBMS. (The terms
*data model* and *database model* are often used interchangeably, but in this article
we use *data model* for the design of a specific database, and *database model* for
the modeling notation used to express that design).

The most popular database model for general-purpose databases is the relational model,
or more precisely, the relational model as represented by the SQL language. The process
of creating a logical database design using this model uses a methodical approach
known as [normalization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). The goal of normalization is to ensure that each elementary "fact" is only recorded in one place, so that insertions, updates, and deletions automatically maintain consistency.

The final stage of database design is to make the decisions that affect performance,
scalability, recovery, security, and the like, which depend on the particular DBMS.
This is often called *physical database design*, and the output is the [physical data model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). A key goal during this stage is [data independence](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), meaning that the decisions made for performance optimization purposes should be invisible to end-users and applications. There are two types of data independence: Physical data independence and logical data independence. Physical design is driven mainly by performance requirements, and requires a good knowledge of the expected workload and access patterns, and a deep understanding of the features offered by the chosen DBMS.

Another aspect of physical database design is security. It involves both defining
[access control](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to database objects as well as defining security levels and methods for the data itself.


### Models

Main article: [Database model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Collage of five types of database models
A database model is a type of data model that determines the logical structure of
a database and fundamentally determines in which manner [data](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") can be stored, organized, and manipulated. The most popular example of a database model is the relational model (or the SQL approximation of relational), which uses a table-based format.

Common logical data models for databases include:

- [Navigational databases](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")- [Hierarchical database model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Network model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Graph database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Relational model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Entity–relationship model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Enhanced entity–relationship model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Object model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Document model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Entity–attribute–value model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Star schema](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

An object–relational database combines the two related structures.

[Physical data models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") include:

- [Inverted index](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Flat file](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Other models include:

- [Multidimensional model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Array model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Multivalue model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")

Specialized models are optimized for particular types of data:

- [XML database](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Semantic model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Content store](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Event store](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
- [Time series model](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")


### External, conceptual, and internal views
[![application/atom+xml](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:CentralAutoLogin/start?useformat=desktop&type=script&usesul3=1 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
Traditional view of data[[43]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
A database management system provides three views of the database data:

- The **external level** defines how each group of end-users sees the organization of data in the database. A single database can have any number of views at the external level.
- The **conceptual level** (or *logical level*) unifies the various external views into a compatible global view.[[44]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") It provides the synthesis of all the external views. It is out of the scope of the various database end-users, and is rather of interest to database application developers and database administrators.
- The **internal level** (or *physical level*) is the internal organization of data inside a DBMS. It is concerned with cost, performance, scalability and other operational matters. It deals with storage layout of the data, using storage structures such as [indexes](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") to enhance performance. Occasionally it stores data of individual views ([materialized views](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")), computed from generic data, if performance justification exists for such redundancy. It balances all the external views' performance requirements, possibly conflicting, in an attempt to optimize overall performance across all activities.

While there is typically only one conceptual and internal view of the data, there
can be any number of different external views. This allows users to see database
information in a more business-related way rather than from a technical, processing
viewpoint. For example, a financial department of a company needs the payment details
of all employees as part of the company's expenses, but does not need details about
employees that are in the interest of the [human resources](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") department. Thus different departments need different
*views* of the company's database.

The three-level database architecture relates to the concept of *data independence*
which was one of the major initial driving forces of the relational model.[[44]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
The idea is that changes made at a certain level do not affect the view at a higher
level. For example, changes in the internal level do not affect application programs
written using conceptual level interfaces, which reduces the impact of making physical
changes to improve performance.

The conceptual view provides a level of indirection between internal and external.
On the one hand it provides a common view of the database, independent of different
external view structures, and on the other hand it abstracts away details of how
the data are stored or managed (internal level). In principle every level, and even
every external view, can be presented by a different data model. In practice usually
a given DBMS uses the same data model for both the external and the conceptual levels
(e.g., relational model). The internal level, which is hidden inside the DBMS and
depends on its implementation, requires a different level of detail and uses its
own types of data structure types.


## Research

Database technology has been an active research topic since the 1960s, both in academia
and in the research and development groups of companies (for example [IBM Research](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")). Research activity includes [theory](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") and development of [prototypes](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("). Notable research topics have included [models](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), the atomic transaction concept, related [concurrency control](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") techniques, query languages and [query optimization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") methods, [RAID](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), and more.

The database research area has several dedicated [academic journals](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (for example, *[ACM Transactions on Database Systems](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*-TODS,
*[Data and Knowledge Engineering](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022
")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")*-DKE)
and annual [conferences](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") (e.g., [ACM](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") [SIGMOD](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), ACM [PODS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), VLDB, [IEEE](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(") ICDE).


Since DBMSs comprise a significant [market](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url("), computer and storage vendors often take into account DBMS requirements in their own development
plans.[[45]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 ")}.oo-ui-image-invert.oo-ui-icon-edit,.mw-ui-icon-edit-invert:before{background-image:url(")
